Runtime equality, hidden-target behavior and resource ceilings remain in force.
@@ -5596,7 +5596,7 @@ boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
 atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.
 
 Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
-the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. It enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules for either mode, before
+the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
 moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
 15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
 16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).
@@ -5608,6 +5608,10 @@ Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
 advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
 reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
 for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
+Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
+per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
+skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
+Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
 Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
 An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
 shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
@@ -8574,3 +8578,14 @@ heading; curated cross-card routing keeps its explanation. Quoted and
 unquoted OR searches print matching lines and are tested at the real CLI.
 Saved duplicate table populations receive a saved-copy hint; JSON stdout
 remains machine-readable. No new language or planner behavior is introduced.
+
+### 20.12.2 Wave 15 clock evidence (KIT4, 2026-10-18)
+
+Implementer: Codex. A coalesced replay command includes --port with the actual
+registered development listener, including memory hosts. An each-boundary
+reason names every fired window. offsetMs and clockOffsetMs report the exact
+requested process offset (initial offset plus accepted advances; reset clears
+it); skewMs remains the observed authoritative now minus sampled wall time.
+Thus ordinary elapsed milliseconds and refused advances cannot change the
+reported configured offset. The durable disclosure floor still governs now.
+Neither job authority, production scheduling nor expiry semantics change.

 ## 1. Contract
@@ -644,3 +644,40 @@ The [identical blind brief](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.brief.
 and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
 `e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
 in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.
+
+## 12. Wave 15 proof diagnostics and clock sampling (KIT4)
+
+Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-18, commissioned for 0.4.32. On a canonical
+argument mismatch the shared E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED message names the differing
+top-level keys. withBrowser installs a diagnostic binding which writes the
+full armed and actual arguments to a fresh mode-0600 *.witness.private.jsonl
+file under the proof log directory. Receipt messages contain no argument values.
+Diagnostic failure never cancels, retries or blocks the application's write.
+This supersedes §11's suppression of argument values in all retained logs;
+private proof logs intentionally contain sensitive application evidence.
+
+Substring timeout advice requires an explicitly scoped renderedText or
+waitForFresh locator and a substring within that scope. Whole-page prose
+cannot trigger the hint. A timeout includes up to three visible page headings
+(300 characters total); it does not weaken exact matching or ambiguity errors.
+
+waitForClock(page, advancedNow, {timeoutMs=30000}) polls the registered
+witness's signed-in client.now(), returning its number once it reaches the
+advance reply's now. It never sets page/driver time or fetches through a
+separate clock. Missing witness/auth, invalid time and the bounded timeout
+refuse explicitly. This is clock observation, not proof of business settlement.
+
+loadFixtures validates options.url before URL parsing and names the rejected
+persona or SHA-256 credential fingerprint without printing bearer bytes.
+startStatic names build:web or build from the project package scripts when
+its output directory is absent. withBrowser resolves playwright-core first,
+with an actionable missing-peer refusal before creating logs or launching a
+browser; the full day-two recipe enters withBrowser before build/deploy.
+
+The source-generated proof API table includes every runtime and type export,
+signatures, result fields and @throws reasons. The packed qualification executes
+the literal wire blocks and production-account day-two script unchanged, plus
+a 30-day two-schedule coalesced advance, explicit cap refusal, memory-host replay
+and array alias case. The production starter's web select labels use explicit
+htmlFor/id pairs. Production entry/auth UI behavior remains with its owning
+production workstream; this amendment changes those label associations only.

new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..bd16ec5698
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/wire-first.test.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,31 @@
+import test from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import {mkdtempSync,mkdirSync,readFileSync,writeFileSync,symlinkSync,rmSync} from 'node:fs';
+import {join,resolve} from 'node:path';
+import {spawnSync} from 'node:child_process';
+const root=resolve(import.meta.dirname,'../../..');
+const binary=process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN??join(root,'target/debug/snapback4');
+const installed=process.env.SNAPBACK4_PROOF_PACKAGE??join(root,'packages/snapback4');
+const source=join(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/wire.md');
+const block=(text,name,language)=>text.match(new RegExp(`<!-- WIRE FIRST ${name} BEGIN -->\\n\x60\x60\x60${language}\\n([\\s\\S]*?)\\n\x60\x60\x60\\n<!-- WIRE FIRST ${name} END -->`))?.[1];
+test('KIT4 literal first wire page runs unchanged and its trace is generated from real requests',{timeout:60000},t=>{
+  let guide;
+  if(process.env.KIT4_UPDATE_WIRE==='1') guide=readFileSync(source,'utf8');
+  else {const result=spawnSync(binary,['guide','wire','--full'],{encoding:'utf8',timeout:10000});assert.equal(result.status,0,result.stderr);guide=result.stdout;}
+  const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(root,'target/kit4-wire-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));
+  mkdirSync(join(cwd,'snapback'));mkdirSync(join(cwd,'node_modules'));
+  symlinkSync(installed,join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4'),'dir');
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),'{"type":"module"}');
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),block(guide,'SCHEMA','quarry'));
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'wire-proof.mjs'),block(guide,'SCRIPT','js'));
+  const result=spawnSync(process.execPath,['wire-proof.mjs'],{cwd,env:{...process.env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:binary},encoding:'utf8',timeout:45000});
+  assert.equal(result.status,0,result.stdout+result.stderr);
+  const trace=JSON.parse(result.stdout);
+  assert.equal(trace.length,7);assert.equal(trace[6].response.status,200);
+  if(process.env.KIT4_UPDATE_WIRE==='1') {
+    guide=guide.replace(/<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE BEGIN -->[\s\S]*?<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE END -->/,
+      '<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE BEGIN -->\n```json\n'+trace.map(row=>JSON.stringify(row)).join(',\n').replace(/^/,'[\n')+'\n]\n```\n<!-- WIRE FIRST TRACE END -->');
+    writeFileSync(source,guide);
+  }
+  assert.deepEqual(trace,JSON.parse(block(guide,'TRACE','json')));
+});
diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/generate-proof-api.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/generate-proof-api.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..7e1690cf96
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/generate-proof-api.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,43 @@
+// The proof export census, signatures, result fields and failures come from TypeScript.
+import ts from 'typescript';
+import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { resolve } from 'node:path';
+const root=fileURLToPath(new URL('../',import.meta.url));
+export function proofApi() {
+  const config=ts.readConfigFile(resolve(root,'packages/snapback4/tsconfig.build.json'),ts.sys.readFile);
+  const parsed=ts.parseJsonConfigFileContent(config.config,ts.sys,resolve(root,'packages/snapback4'));
+  const program=ts.createProgram(parsed.fileNames,parsed.options), checker=program.getTypeChecker();
+  const source=program.getSourceFile(resolve(root,'packages/snapback4/src/proof.ts'));
+  const module=checker.getSymbolAtLocation(source);
+  const fmt=ts.TypeFormatFlags.NoTruncation | ts.TypeFormatFlags.UseAliasDefinedOutsideCurrentScope;
+  const show=type=>checker.typeToString(type,source,fmt).replace(/import\("[^"]+"\)\./g,'');
+  const cell=text=>text.replace(/\|/g,'&#124;').replace(/\s+/g,' ').trim();
+  const fields=type=>{
+    type=checker.getAwaitedType(type)??type;
+    if(checker.isArrayType(type)) return `Array of ${fields(checker.getTypeArguments(type)[0])}`;
+    if(type.isUnion()) return type.types.map(fields).join(' or ');
+    const name=show(type);
+    if(!['ProofHost','DevHost','CliResult','TestSourceResult','FixtureRow'].includes(name)) return name;
+    return `{ ${checker.getPropertiesOfType(type).map(p=>`${p.name}${p.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Optional?'?':''}: ${show(checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(p,source))}`).join('; ')} }`;
+  };
+  const functions=[],types=[];
+  for(let symbol of checker.getExportsOfModule(module).sort((a,b)=>a.name.localeCompare(b.name))) {
+    const name=symbol.name;
+    if(symbol.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Alias) symbol=checker.getAliasedSymbol(symbol);
+    const type=checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(symbol,source), signatures=type.getCallSignatures();
+    if(signatures.length){
+      const failures=symbol.getJsDocTags(checker).filter(tag=>tag.name==='throws').map(tag=>tag.text?.map(part=>part.text).join('')??'').join('; ');
+      if(!failures) throw Error(`proof export ${name} needs source @throws documentation`);
+      functions.push(`| ${signatures.map(sig=>'`'+cell(name+checker.signatureToString(sig,source,fmt))+'`').join('<br>')} | \`${cell(fields(checker.getReturnTypeOfSignature(signatures[0])))}\` | ${cell(failures)} |`);
+    } else {
+      const declared=checker.getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol(symbol);
+      types.push(`| \`${name}\` | \`${cell(fields(declared)===''+name ? checker.getPropertiesOfType(declared).map(p=>`${p.name}${p.flags&ts.SymbolFlags.Optional?'?':''}: ${show(checker.getTypeOfSymbolAtLocation(p,source))}`).join('; ') : fields(declared))}\` |`);
+    }
+  }
+  return '## Proof API (generated from source)\n\nEvery runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.\n\n| Signature | Result / fields | Failure reasons |\n|---|---|---|\n'+functions.join('\n')+'\n\n| Exported type | Fields |\n|---|---|\n'+types.join('\n')+'\n';
+}
+const path=resolve(root,'crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/proof_api.md');
+const output=proofApi();
+if(process.argv.includes('--check')) {if(readFileSync(path,'utf8')!==output) throw Error('proof API table is stale; run node scripts/generate-proof-api.mjs');}
+else writeFileSync(path,output);

 }
diff --git a/snapback4/scripts/publication-proof-kit.mjs b/snapback4/scripts/publication-proof-kit.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..743de5990d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/scripts/publication-proof-kit.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,90 @@
+// Packed guide recipes and pre-wave controls. Every example executes unchanged.
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import {mkdirSync,readFileSync,writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';
+import {join,resolve} from 'node:path';
+import {spawnSync} from 'node:child_process';
+import {fileURLToPath} from 'node:url';
+import {runToolchainCommand} from './publication-starters.mjs';
+import {chromiumExecutable} from '../witness/lib.mjs';
+const root=fileURLToPath(new URL('../',import.meta.url));
+export async function proveProofKit(client,platform,directory) {
+  const cwd=join(resolve(directory),'proof-kit');mkdirSync(cwd,{recursive:true});
+  const env={...process.env,CI:'1',CHROME:process.env.CHROME??chromiumExecutable(),npm_config_audit:'false',npm_config_fund:'false'};
+  delete env.SNAPBACK4_BIN;delete env.PORT_LO;delete env.PORT_HI;
+  const run=(command,args)=>runToolchainCommand(command,args,{cwd,env,timeout:300000});
+  const capture=args=>{
+    const result=spawnSync(process.execPath,[join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4/bin/snapback4.js'),...args],{cwd,env,encoding:'utf8',timeout:30000});
+    assert.equal(result.status,0,result.stdout+result.stderr);return result.stdout;
+  };
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),'{"private":true,"type":"module"}');
+  await run('npm',['install','--ignore-scripts','--no-audit','--no-fund',resolve(client),resolve(platform)]);
+  const wrapper=join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4/bin/snapback4.js');
+  await run(process.execPath,[wrapper,'init','--web']);
+  const pkg=JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json')));
+  pkg.dependencies.snapback4=`file:${resolve(client)}`;
+  pkg.optionalDependencies={[`snapback4-${process.platform}-${process.arch}`]:`file:${resolve(platform)}`};
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'package.json'),JSON.stringify(pkg,null,2));
+  await run('npm',['install','--prefer-offline','--no-audit','--no-fund']);
+  env.PATH=join(cwd,'node_modules/.bin')+':'+env.PATH;
+  const guide=capture(['guide','testing','--full']);writeFileSync(join(cwd,'testing-guide.md'),guide);
+  const recipe=guide.match(/<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS BEGIN -->\n```js\n([\s\S]*?)\n```\n<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS END -->/)?.[1];
+  assert.ok(recipe,'packed guide has the complete day-two proof');
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'day2-accounts.mjs'),recipe);
+  await run(process.execPath,['day2-accounts.mjs']);
+  writeFileSync(join(cwd,'clock-controls.mjs'),clockControls);
+  await run(process.execPath,['clock-controls.mjs']);
+  const testEnv={...env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:join(cwd,`node_modules/snapback4-${process.platform}-${process.arch}/snapback4`),SNAPBACK4_PROOF_PACKAGE:join(cwd,'node_modules/snapback4')};
+  await runToolchainCommand(process.execPath,['--test',join(root,'packages/snapback4/test/wire-first.test.mjs')],{cwd,env:testEnv,timeout:60000});
+  return {status:'passed',dayTwo:'production accounts, UI sign-in, v1/hot v2/rollback/forward business assertions',wire:'literal first section, actual packed CLI trace',clock:'30-day coalesced jump, each-boundary cap, memory replay command, page sampling',aliases:'array index path through packed test'};
+}
+const clockControls=String.raw`
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import {mkdtemp,mkdir,writeFile,rm} from 'node:fs/promises';
+import {tmpdir} from 'node:os';
+import {join} from 'node:path';
+import {startDev,startStatic,withBrowser,waitForClock,renderedText,waitForFresh,cli,command,cleanup} from 'snapback4/proof';
+const cwd=await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(),'kit4-packed-clock-'));
+let dev,web,uiDev;
+try {
+  await mkdir(join(cwd,'snapback'));await mkdir(join(cwd,'fixtures'));
+  const source="use personas alice\ntable counters:\n  n: int\n  cap 1\n  public 'proof'\n  update <- allow\n  online only\nquery counts():\n  return counters first 1 by .id\nmutation bump(counterId: counters):\n  row = counters[counterId] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  update counters[row] {n:row.n+1}\n  return null\njob tick():\n  chunk row in counters first 1 by .id:\n    update counters[row.id] {n:row.n+1}\njob secondTick():\n  chunk row in counters first 1 by .id:\n    update counters[row.id] {n:row.n+1}\nschedule first every 5m: tick\nschedule second every 5m: secondTick\n";
+  await writeFile(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),source);
+  await writeFile(join(cwd,'fixtures/counters.jsonl'),'{"id":"counter","n":0}\n');
+  await writeFile(join(cwd,'fixtures/test.cases.jsonl'),[
+    {as:'alice',op:'counts',args:{},save:'rows',count:1},
+    {as:'alice',op:'bump',args:{counterId:'$rows.0.id'}},
+    {as:'alice',op:'counts',args:{},match:[{n:1}]}].map(JSON.stringify).join('\n'));
+  const tested=await cli(['test'],{cwd});assert.equal(tested.code,0,tested.stdout+tested.stderr);
+  dev=await startDev({cwd,args:['--no-watch']});
+  const post=async(path,body)=>(await fetch(dev.url+path,{method:'POST',headers:{'x-snapback-persona':'alice'},body:JSON.stringify(body)})).json();
+  await post('/seed',[{table:'counters',row:{id:'counter',n:0}}]);
+  const refused=await post('/dev/clock/advance',{duration:'30d',eachBoundary:true});
+  assert.equal(refused.denied.code,'E_CLOCK_INPUT');assert.match(refused.denied.message,/--each-boundary.*10000.*omit --each-boundary/);
+  assert.equal((await (await fetch(dev.url+'/dev/status')).json()).clockOffsetMs,0);
+  const advanced=await post('/dev/clock/advance',{duration:'30d'});
+  assert.equal(advanced.offsetMs,30*86400000);assert.equal(advanced.schedules.length,2);
+  for(const schedule of advanced.schedules) {assert.equal(schedule.firedWindows.length,1);assert.equal(schedule.skippedPeriods,8639);assert.equal(schedule.skippedBoundariesOmitted,8607);}
+  const count=async()=> (await post('/q/counts',{args:{}})).data[0].n;
+  assert.equal(await count(),2);
+  const replay=advanced.schedules[0].skippedBoundaries[0].command;
+  assert.ok(replay.includes('--port '+new URL(dev.url).port),replay);
+  const replayed=await command('sh',['-c',replay],{cwd});assert.equal(replayed.code,0,replayed.stderr);assert.equal(await count(),3);
+  await dev.stop();dev=undefined;
+  // The built production starter uses its own real client and witness clock.
+  uiDev=await startDev();web=await startStatic('dist');
+  await withBrowser({executablePath:process.env.CHROME},async context=>{
+    const page=await context.newPage();await page.goto(web.url+'/?api='+encodeURIComponent(uiDev.url));
+    await page.getByRole('button',{name:'Continue as a guest',exact:true}).click();
+    await waitForFresh(renderedText(page,'Teams and roles'),null);
+    const response=await fetch(uiDev.url+'/dev/clock/advance',{method:'POST',body:JSON.stringify({duration:'2m'})});
+    const advanced=await response.json();assert.ok(!advanced.denied,JSON.stringify(advanced));
+    assert.ok(await waitForClock(page,advanced.now)>=advanced.now);
+  });
+  console.log('packed clock and alias controls passed');
+} finally {await cleanup(()=>web?.stop(),()=>uiDev?.stop(),()=>dev?.stop(),()=>rm(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));}
+`;
+if(process.argv[1]&&resolve(process.argv[1])===fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) {
+  const [client,platform,out]=process.argv.slice(2);
+  if(!client||!platform||!out) throw Error('usage: publication-proof-kit.mjs client.tgz platform.tgz output-directory');
+  console.log(JSON.stringify(await proveProofKit(client,platform,out),null,2));
+}

## Day-two proof with production accounts

Run this in a fresh `init --web` starter after installing its dependencies.
Save the block as scripts/day2-accounts.mjs and run `node scripts/day2-accounts.mjs`.
It uses the starter's complete production recipe (teams and memberships),
compiles and builds that UI, then signs in through its production account form.
The production entry recipe is maintained on `guide production`.
Adapt the assertions to your own business model when replacing this example.

The proof keeps one live serve process and one browser page through v1 → v2 →
rollback → forward. It tests real password accounts, a rendered durable write,
existing-row defaults, a new membership, authorization refusals, and retained
rows at every transition. Saved sources and deployments belong to its private
scratch directory. It changes the fresh starter's working schema and UI to the
production recipe; run it in a copy when preserving an existing application.

<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS BEGIN -->
```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { copyFile, mkdir, mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile, rm } from "node:fs/promises";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { signup } from "snapback4/auth";
import { withBrowser, command, cli, deploy, rollback, startServe, startStatic,
  fillRN, bounded, renderedText, waitForFresh, waitForServer, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";

await withBrowser({ executablePath: process.env.CHROME }, async context => {
  // Browser dependency checks happen before building or deploying anything.
  for (const [from, to] of [["schema.q", "snapback/schema.q"], ["App.tsx", "src/App.tsx"], ["client.ts", "src/client.ts"]]) {
    await copyFile(`recipes/production/${from}`, to);
  }
  for (const args of [["check"]]) { const result = await cli(args); assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr); }
  for (const script of ["typecheck", "build"]) {
    const result = await command("npm", ["run", script]); assert.equal(result.code, 0, result.stderr);
  }
  const work = await mkdtemp(join(process.cwd(), ".day2-accounts-"));
  let serve, web;
  try {
    const v1 = join(work, "v1"), v2 = join(work, "v2"), directory = join(work, "deployment");
    await mkdir(v1); await mkdir(v2);
    const base = await readFile("recipes/production/schema.q", "utf8");
    const evolved = base.replace("table members:\n", "table members:\n  note: text <=40 default 'evolved'\n");
    assert.notEqual(evolved, base);
    await writeFile(join(v1, "schema.q"), base); await writeFile(join(v2, "schema.q"), evolved);
    await deploy(directory, v1);
    serve = await startServe(directory); web = await startStatic("dist");
    const password = "Proof-password-42!", email = `admin-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`;
    const admin = await signup(serve.url, email, password);
    const member = await signup(serve.url, `member-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`, password);
    const outsider = await signup(serve.url, `outsider-${crypto.randomUUID()}@example.test`, password);
    for (const account of [admin, member, outsider]) assert.equal(account.ok, true, JSON.stringify(account));
    const page = await context.newPage();
    await page.goto(`${web.url}/?api=${encodeURIComponent(serve.url)}`, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Email", { exact: true }), email);
    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Password", { exact: true }), password);
    await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Sign in", exact: true }).click();
    await waitForFresh(renderedText(page, "Teams and roles"), null);
    assert.equal(await page.getByRole("button", { name: /^Dev:/ }).count(), 0, "serve offers accounts only");
    const title = `Team ${crypto.randomUUID()}`;
    await fillRN(page.getByLabel("Team name", { exact: true }), title);
    const create = page.getByRole("button", { name: "Create team", exact: true });
    await create.evaluate((button, title) => window.__snapback4Witness.beginForm("createTeam", { title }, button), title);
    await create.click();
    const receipt = await bounded(page.evaluate(() => window.__snapback4Witness.formResult()), 30000, "team receipt");
    assert.equal(receipt.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(receipt));
    const teamId = receipt.result.id;
    async function call(account, door, name, args) {
      const response = await fetch(`${serve.url}/${door}/${name}`, { method: "POST",
        headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.session.token}` },
        body: JSON.stringify({ ...(door === "m" ? { id: crypto.randomUUID() } : {}), args }) });
      assert.equal(response.status, 200); return response.json();
    }
    async function business(label, evolved, joined) {
      const own = await waitForServer(() => call(admin, "q", "myRole", { teamId }), read => read.complete === true, { label });
      assert.equal(own.data.principal, admin.session.principal); assert.equal(own.data.role, "admin");
      assert.equal(own.data.note, evolved ? "evolved" : undefined, label);
      const roster = await call(admin, "q", "roster", { teamId, c: null });
      assert.equal(roster.complete, true); assert.equal(roster.data.length, joined ? 2 : 1, label);
      assert.ok(roster.data.some(row => row.principal === admin.session.principal));
      if (joined) assert.ok(roster.data.some(row => row.principal === member.session.principal));
      assert.equal((await call(outsider, "q", "myRole", { teamId })).denied.code, "NOT_MEMBER", label);
      const refused = await call(outsider, "m", "invite", { teamId, principal: outsider.session.principal, role: "admin" });
      assert.equal(refused.state, "failed"); assert.equal(refused.why.code, "NOT_ADMIN", label);
      await waitForFresh(renderedText(page, new RegExp(teamId), { within: page.locator("main") }), null);
    }
    await business("v1", false, false);
    await deploy(directory, v2);
    const invited = await call(admin, "m", "invite", { teamId, principal: member.session.principal, role: "member" });
    assert.equal(invited.state, "sent", JSON.stringify(invited));
    await business("v2", true, true);
    await rollback(directory, 1); await business("rollback-v1", false, true);
    await rollback(directory, "latest"); await business("forward-v2", true, true);
    console.log("day-two accounts passed: v1, hot v2, rollback, forward; business assertions at every step");
  } finally { await cleanup(() => web?.stop(), () => serve?.stop(), () => rm(work, { recursive: true, force: true })); }
});
```
<!-- DAY TWO ACCOUNTS END -->
# Wire: HTTP for curl, scripts and non-JS clients

Every call carries a principal; public visitors use guest sessions; `serve` accepts bearers only. Plain JSON over HTTP works with curl, scripts and non-JS clients.

## First raw HTTP proof

| Send | Inspect the JSON body |
|---|---|
| `POST /q/name {"args":{…}}` | `{data, complete}` or `{denied:{…}}` |
| `POST /m/name {"id":"one-intent","args":{…}}` | `{state:"sent", result}` or `{state:"failed", why}` |
| `POST /seed [{"table":"notes","row":{…}}]` | Bare array; `{seeded}` or `{denied}` |
| `POST /dev/reset {"seed":[{"table":"notes","row":{…}}]}` | Wrapper; `{reset:true, seeded, …}` or `{denied}` |

Dev uses `x-snapback-persona: alice`. Serve uses `Authorization: Bearer <token>`
from a real account or service seat. Send one credential with `Content-Type:
application/json`. Authentication can refuse HTTP 401 before either operation;
HTTP 200 still requires inspecting `denied`, `state`, and `why`.
Relative times such as `"now+1h"` work in typed seed/fixture time fields and
fixture case arguments. `/m` takes Unix milliseconds; it never interprets
relative time strings. Query data and mutation results have different envelopes.

Save this schema as snapback/schema.q in a scratch project with snapback4
installed. Save the JavaScript as scripts/wire-proof.mjs and run it with Node.
Qualification executes these exact blocks through the packed CLI and generates
the trace below. Minted identifiers, timestamps and observed durations alone
are normalized; refusal codes, request bodies and envelope fields are retained.

<!-- WIRE FIRST SCHEMA BEGIN -->
```quarry
use personas alice, bob
table notes:
  title: text <=80
  at: time
  public 'literal wire proof'
  insert <- allow
  online only
query list(c: cursor ?):
  require viewer = 'dev:alice' else READ_REFUSED
  return notes first 10 by .id after c
mutation add(title: text <=80, at: time):
  require title != 'refuse' else WRITE_REFUSED
  return insert notes { title, at }
```
<!-- WIRE FIRST SCHEMA END -->

<!-- WIRE FIRST SCRIPT BEGIN -->
```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { startDev, cleanup } from "snapback4/proof";
const dev = await startDev();
const trace = [];
async function post(path, body, persona = "alice") {
  const headers = { "content-type": "application/json", "x-snapback-persona": persona };
  const response = await fetch(dev.url + path, { method: "POST", headers, body: JSON.stringify(body) });
  const result = await response.json();
  trace.push({ request: { method: "POST", path, headers, body }, response: { status: response.status, body: result } });
  return result;
}
try {
  const seed = [{ table: "notes", row: { id: "seed", title: "Seed", at: "now+1h" } }];
  assert.equal((await post("/seed", seed)).seeded, 1);
  const reset = await post("/dev/reset", { seed });
  assert.equal(reset.reset, true); assert.notEqual(reset.storeId, reset.previousStoreId);
  const at = await dev.now();
  const sent = await post("/m/add", { id: "proof-write", args: { title: "Proof", at } });
  assert.equal(sent.state, "sent"); assert.equal(sent.id, "proof-write"); assert.ok(sent.result.id);
  const failed = await post("/m/add", { id: "refused-write", args: { title: "refuse", at } });
  assert.equal(failed.state, "failed"); assert.equal(failed.why.code, "WRITE_REFUSED");
  const read = await post("/q/list", { args: { c: null } });
  assert.equal(read.complete, true); assert.equal(read.data.length, 2);
  assert.equal((await post("/q/list", { args: { c: null } }, "bob")).denied.code, "READ_REFUSED");
  assert.equal((await post("/m/add", { args: { title: "Relative", at: "now+1h" } })).why.code, "E_INPUT");
  const normalize = (value, key = "") => {
    if (Array.isArray(value)) return value.map(item => normalize(item));
    if (value && typeof value === "object") return Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(value).map(([k,v]) => [k, normalize(v,k)]));
    if (["store_id", "storeId", "previousStoreId"].includes(key)) return "<store-id>";
    if (key === "id" && !["seed", "proof-write", "refused-write"].includes(value)) return "<row-id>";
    if (["ms", "skewMs"].includes(key)) return "<observed-ms>";
    return typeof value === "number" && value > 1_000_000_000_000 ? "<unix-ms>" : value;
  };
  console.log(JSON.stringify(normalize(trace), null, 2));
} finally { await cleanup(() => dev.stop()); }
```
<!-- WIRE FIRST SCRIPT END -->

## Identity

/auth/config includes development true on dev and false on serve; show persona controls only in development.

In development, `x-snapback-persona: alice` acts as `dev:alice`: same machine
only, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, and only for a name `use personas` declares; any other
name is 401 E_AUTH naming the declaration (a schema without `use personas`
admits any name). `snapback4 serve` refuses personas. Everywhere else send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, from
`/auth/guest` (`{}`), `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login` (`{"email","password"}`),
each answering `{"session":{principal,kind,token,expiresAt}}`; a worker's token
comes from `snapback4 token --service <name>`. Send one credential, not both.

## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}` or `{loading:true,data:null,complete:false,…}`; loading data is a placeholder, never an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. Check `loading` and `denied` before `data`; HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

## Literal query and mutation replies

The fixture and complete replies below are generated by a real dev run in
qualification, using the installed package's binary. Only minted row/store ids
are normalized to row1/store1. All six calls return HTTP 200: inspect the body.
`list` as alice succeeds; the same query as bob refuses under `denied`.
`add` succeeds with title Proof, and refuses title refuse under `state`/`why`.
A point lookup (`one`) returns one object or null, never a one-element list.
A view scan (`countRows`) returns list rows with `value`; a keyed aggregate
lookup returns its scalar value. Query and mutation envelopes are distinct.

<!-- WIRE ENVELOPES BEGIN -->
```quarry
use personas alice, bob
table notes:
  owner: principal
  title: text <=80
  public 'wire recipe'
  insert <- allow
  online only
view noteCounts: count notes by .owner
query list(c: cursor ?):
  require viewer = 'dev:alice' else READ_REFUSED
  return notes first 10 by .id after c
query one(id: notes):
  return notes[id]
query countRows(c: cursor ?):
  return noteCounts first 10 by .owner after c { owner, value }
mutation add(title: text <=80):
  require title != 'refuse' else WRITE_REFUSED
  return insert notes { owner: viewer, title }
```

```json
{
  "/m/add Proof": {"status":200,"body":{"generation":1,"head":1,"preserved":["notes"],"result":{"id":"row1","owner":"dev:alice","title":"Proof"},"seq":1,"state":"sent","store_id":"store1"}},
  "/m/add refuse": {"status":200,"body":{"generation":1,"head":1,"preserved":[],"state":"failed","store_id":"store1","why":{"code":"WRITE_REFUSED","family":"rule","guide":"programs","message":"the program refused with WRITE_REFUSED","rule":"WRITE_REFUSED","site":"schema.q:17:3"}}},
  "/q/list alice": {"status":200,"body":{"scopes":{},"generation":1,"complete":true,"cut":null,"data":[{"id":"row1","owner":"dev:alice","title":"Proof"}],"next":null,"nextExpiry":null,"seq":1,"store_id":"store1","tables":["notes"],"viewer":"dev:alice","meter":{"cut":null,"examined":1,"heapBytes":350,"indexEntries":0,"outputBytes":116,"probes":0,"rowsWritten":0,"steps":7}}},
  "/q/list bob": {"status":200,"body":{"complete":false,"denied":{"code":"READ_REFUSED","family":"rule","guide":"programs","message":"the program refused with READ_REFUSED","rule":"READ_REFUSED","site":"schema.q:10:3"},"generation":1,"head":1,"seq":1,"store_id":"store1"}},
  "/q/one alice": {"status":200,"body":{"scopes":{},"generation":1,"complete":true,"cut":null,"data":{"id":"row1","owner":"dev:alice","title":"Proof"},"next":null,"nextExpiry":null,"seq":1,"store_id":"store1","tables":["notes"],"viewer":"dev:alice","meter":{"cut":null,"examined":1,"heapBytes":716,"indexEntries":0,"outputBytes":104,"probes":0,"rowsWritten":0,"steps":3}}},
  "/q/countRows alice": {"status":200,"body":{"scopes":{},"generation":1,"complete":true,"cut":null,"data":[{"owner":"dev:alice","value":1}],"next":null,"nextExpiry":null,"seq":1,"store_id":"store1","tables":["noteCounts"],"viewer":"dev:alice","meter":{"cut":null,"examined":1,"heapBytes":1165,"indexEntries":0,"outputBytes":66,"probes":0,"rowsWritten":0,"steps":13}}}
}
```
<!-- WIRE ENVELOPES END -->

## Operations

- `/q/<op>` with `{"args":{..}}` answers `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer}`
  or `{denied}`. `data` is what the query returns, as is: a list, a row, a shape,
  or a scalar (a keyed view read `totals[owner]` answers the number itself).
  Page by passing `next` back as the query's cursor argument.
- `/m/<op>` with `{"id"?, "args":{..}}` answers `{state:"sent", id, seq, result}` or
  `{state:"failed", id, why, current?}`, both HTTP 200 (see Refusals for the
  status codes). `result` is what the mutation returns (an inserted row's id is
  `result.id`); the top-level `id` is the write id you sent, omitted without one. With an `id` (a unique string the caller mints once per
  intended write), a retry of the same call applies at most once and answers
  `replayed: true`; the same `id` with another operation or other arguments is
  `{state:"failed", why:{code:"E_ID_REUSED"}}`. Without an `id`, every POST is a
  new write.
  A replay omits `result` and `current`: the durable receipt retains execution
  identity, not historical values or read authority. Read current data with a
  query instead of treating an absent replay result as a new-write result.
- Values: `time` is Unix milliseconds; `bytes` is lowercase hex; a reference is
  its row's id string. Numbers: see below.
- Uploads: `POST /assets` with the file's bytes as the raw body (not JSON, not multipart; the
  server sniffs JPEG, PNG, WebP or MP4 and ignores `Content-Type`), as the uploader, answers
  `{asset: {id, type, bytes, width, height, duration}}`, or `{denied}`: `E_ASSET_TYPE` (other
  bytes), `E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE`, 400 for an empty body, 401 `E_AUTH`, 413 past 64 MiB. Pass the
  asset to a mutation's `image`/`video`/`file` argument as `{"id": "<asset id>"}`; `GET /assets/<id>`
  returns the bytes to a viewer the row's read rule admits, else 404 `E_ASSET_UNKNOWN`.
  `curl -X POST $U/assets -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' --data-binary @receipt.png`
- A job is not a client call (E_OP): a schedule fires it, and in dev
  `/dev/jobs/<job>` or `snapback4 run <job>` runs one.
- GET `/changes?since=<seq>&wait=25` long-polls. It answers `{seq, tables,
  generation, store_id}` when a commit lands or after `wait` seconds: re-query
  what reads those tables, then poll again from the new `seq`. `/sync` is the
  device replication protocol; use the client rather than reimplementing it.
- A held wait is bounded. A full host answers 503 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 2);
  one principal past its share of held polls (one per tab or device, default
  16) gets 429 `E_STREAMS` (Retry-After: 5). Both are retryable and admitted
  nothing: wait for Retry-After, poll `/changes?since=<seq>` without `wait`
  meanwhile, then hold again. The JS client does this for you.
- A host that runs the device itself (the native module or the wasm device
  without `snapback4/client`) must route each device `query` reply: some say
  "ask the server" (`unknown` without `retained`) and carry a placeholder
  `data`. "The device reply" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §7 is that contract.

## Numbers, money and decimals

Every number on the wire is a JSON integer within ±(2^53 − 1); there are no
floats. `money CUR` (scale 2 unless given) and `decimal S` hold an integer
scaled by 10^S and travel as that integer, in both directions:

```quarry
table expenses:
  owner: principal
  amount: money USD                 -- $12.50 travels as 1250
  rate: decimal 4                   -- 0.0725 travels as 725
  place: json <=400 ?               -- numbers inside are integers too
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
mutation addExpense(amount: money USD, rate: decimal 4, place: json <=400 ?):
  require amount > 0 else BAD_AMOUNT
  return insert expenses { owner: viewer, amount, rate, place }
query spent():
  return sum(expenses where .owner = viewer, .amount)
```

```sh
curl -s -X POST $U/m/addExpense -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"args":{"amount":1250,"rate":725,"place":{"latE6":37774900,"lngE6":-122419400}}}'
```

`spent` answers `{"data":1250,..}`: cents, not dollars. `12.5` and `1000.0`
refuse HTTP 400 E_INPUT ("argument amount: an integer"), `"12.50"` too ("expected
an integer amount in minor units"), and so does a fraction anywhere inside a
`json` value ("a number must be an integer in the signed 53-bit range"). Scale with rounding before sending:
`Math.round(dollars * 100)`, since `0.29 * 100` is `28.999999999999996`; in
Python send `round(x * 100)`, because `json.dumps(10.0)` writes `10.0`.
Format on display, `(cents / 100).toFixed(2)`. Keep coordinates and
measurements as scaled integers (microdegrees, milligrams) or as text.

## Development doors

Dev only, same machine; `serve` refuses each one.

- `/seed` with a JSON array `[{"table":"t","row":{"id":"..",..}}, ..]` writes
  every row as the system in one commit, rules bypassed (types, constraints,
  caps and sync still apply), and answers `{seeded: N}`; anything else is 400
  E_INPUT, and a row the engine refuses answers 200 `{denied}` with nothing
  written. Rows are written like inserts: omitted columns take their
  `default` (`now` is the dev clock), sequence columns are assigned and must be
  omitted (E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED), and the `$`-prefixed fields `snapback4 data`
  prints are recorded by the engine itself: drop them before seeding. It is an upsert by `id`: every row
  names its own (any unique string; `idFrom(label)` gives a stable one), a
  reseed updates the mutable columns of an existing row, and a different value
  for an `immutable` column refuses E_CONSTRAINT ("… is immutable; /seed is an
  upsert by id …"): keep the original value, use a new id, `POST /dev/reset`
  first, or restart dev with `--memory`. A principal field holds `"dev:alice"`,
  not `"alice"`.
- `/dev/reset` empties the store for the next test, in one transaction: every
  application row, view and maintained column, the sync log, the effect, job
  and action queues, uploaded assets, and accounts with their sessions
  (signed-up users, guests). The adopted schema, personas and service seats
  (`snapback4 token`, the dev worker's) stay. The store takes a new identity:
  a local client re-acquires the new store by itself, an online client halts
  with `E_STORE_REPLACED` (`createApp` reopens it). The head keeps counting.
  The body is empty, a `/seed` array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}`
  (either key optional): seed rows are written as `/seed` writes them, in the
  same transaction (a refused row resets nothing), and `"clock": "reset"`
  drops `--clock-offset` and every advance (by default the dev clock stays).
  It answers `{reset: true, seeded: N, generation, storeId, previousStoreId,
  head, now, skewMs, clockOffsetMs, ms}` in a few milliseconds on a small store;
  `snapback4 reset [--port N] [--seed fixtures] [--clock reset]` posts it.
- `/dev/clock/advance` with `{"duration":"+2m"}` moves the dev clock, sweeps
  expiry, fires due schedules and runs queued jobs (`snapback4 clock advance 2m`).
  The reply includes now, skewMs and each schedule’s firedWindows and skippedBoundaries,
  with a literal run command per skipped job window. Add eachBoundary:true
  (`clock advance 2m --each-boundary`) to admit every crossed window in order.
  Jobs see the final advanced now; use periodStart/periodEnd for historical windows.
- `/dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}` runs one job now and answers its run.
- GET `/dev/status` (no credential) is the readiness probe: it names the
  server's `pid`, `project`, whether the store is `temporary`, the served
  generation, the dev clock and the declared personas.
- `snapback4 run <op> --as bob` beside a running dev forwards to these routes.

## Refusals

| HTTP | Meaning / body |
|---|---|
| 200 | Operation success, or decoded application refusal (`denied` / `state:"failed", why`); inspect the body |
| 202 | An admitted asynchronous action is still running |
| 400 | Malformed JSON/wire arguments or wrong operation door, `denied` |
| 401 | Missing/dead credential, production persona, failed login or disabled identity method, `denied` E_AUTH |
| 403 | Development door on serve, read-only token attempting a write, or supervised-worker refresh, `denied` |
| 404 | Unknown route/operation, or hidden/missing asset, `denied` |
| 413 | Request exceeds the body ceiling, `denied` |
| 429 | Identity rate limit or per-principal wait capacity; retry as directed |
| 500 | Internal store/response-metadata failure, `denied` |
| 503 | Retryable refusal, store/infrastructure failure or host capacity; retry as directed |

Authentication refuses before an operation executes. Identity helpers in the
JS client preserve the `denied` body on 401/403; do not replace it with a proxy
error page. Rule/constraint refusals after operation admission remain HTTP 200.
Identity scope/value validation can also return 200 denied/E_INPUT; inspect the body.
After a streaming heartbeat starts, HTTP stays 200; inspect the final JSON for
`denied` if the session expires while waiting.

Two layers answer differently. The HTTP layer decodes the request against the
operation's signature before anything runs: a body that is not JSON, an
argument of the wrong JSON type or shape (a fraction, a string for an `int`,
a number for `text`), or an argument the operation does not take is **400**
`{denied:{code:"E_INPUT"}}`. No or a dead credential (or an undeclared
persona) is **401** E_AUTH; an unknown operation or route is **404** E_OP, and
a declared one at the wrong door (a job or query at `/m/`, a mutation at
`/q/`) is **400** E_OP naming the right one; a body past its ceiling (1 MiB of JSON, 16 MiB for `/seed` and `/dev/reset`) is **413**. Once
decoded, the operation runs, and everything it refuses is **200**: a query
answers `{denied}`, a mutation `{state:"failed", why}`. That includes value
checks made while it runs — a missing required argument, text outside its
bounds, `''` for a `text 1..500` argument too (`why.code` E_INPUT) — rules (E_RULE), constraints
(E_CONSTRAINT), a delete that rows still reference (E_REFERENCED, naming the
referencing column), `NOT_FOUND`, the program's own `require … else CODE` (with
`why.site` and `why.rule`), and a reused write id (E_ID_REUSED). A retryable
refusal is 503 instead. So branch on the body, not only the status; for an
`add(body: text <=200)` mutation:

```sh
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":12.5}}'
# {"denied":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"argument body: expected text …"}} 400
curl -s -w ' %{http_code}\n' -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -d '{"args":{"body":"'"$(printf 'x%.0s' {1..300})"'"}}'
# {"state":"failed","why":{"code":"E_INPUT","message":"E_INPUT: body is longer than 200 characters"}} 200
```

A refusal is `{code, family, message, site?, rule?, rewrite?, guide?}`, and
`snapback4 why <code>` explains one. `E_WRITE_ID_REUSE` is the JS client's own
local refusal for the same mistake, before anything is sent; the wire's code is
`E_ID_REUSED`.

## Uploads and downloads

`POST /assets` takes the raw bytes as the body and answers `{"asset":{id,type,bytes,…}}`
or `{denied}`; a mutation then places `{"id": …}` in its `image`, `video` or `file`
argument. An image or video is sniffed from its bytes. A document for a `file`
column adds `?as=file&name=<percent-encoded filename>` and declares its type,
which the server checks against the bytes (`guide assets`, "Files"):

```sh
curl -s -X POST "$U/assets?as=file&name=March%20receipt.pdf" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/pdf' --data-binary @receipt.pdf
# {"asset":{"id":"…","type":"application/pdf","bytes":48213,"name":"March receipt.pdf","width":null,"height":null,"duration":null}}
curl -s -OJ "$U/assets/<id>" -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice'   # Content-Disposition: attachment names the file
```

`GET /assets/<id>` is 404 E_ASSET_UNKNOWN when hidden or gone; a file always
arrives as an attachment, and `Range` works on ordinary assets.

The full reference, including the rest of `/auth`, worker and action
doors, and clock headers, is "The wire" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §5, which
ships in the package beside README.md.


## Device engine compatibility

A device sends `x-snapback-device` on `/schema`, every `/sync`, and every
`/changes`: JSON `{format:1,version:"0.4.24",capabilities:[...]}` from the
actual Rust engine, available before opening a store. Memory, SQLite, wasm
and Expo native report the same format; do not use the JS package version.
`device --engine-info`, wasm `engine_info()` and native `sb4_engine_info()`
expose it. Forward `engineInfo()` when wrapping a client device opener.

Every release writes `device-compatibility.json`; `/schema` returns its active
`device_compatibility:{server_version,minimum_version,capabilities}`.
The initial floor is 0.4.24. Required capabilities include
`sync-generation-v1` and the active public schema's capability set. A newer
version still needs every required capability.

After authentication, an incompatible device receives HTTP 409
`{denied:{code:"E_DEVICE_VERSION",family:"device-version",message,...}}`
before any sync body or backend is decoded. The message names the device
and server versions, missing capability, floor and rebuild commands:
Exact `bun exact.mjs snapback --rebuild`, Expo `npx expo run:ios`, web reinstall the
server's `snapback4` version and rebuild. Unstamped devices have unknown
version. `/sync` requires the handshake; a bare online/one-shot `/changes`
or `/schema` caller has no replica and needs none.

The local JS client exports `DeviceVersionError` with
`status:{state:"incompatible",why}` at startup. A running client exposes
`deviceVersion()` and `onDeviceVersion(status)`. An incompatible sync stops
replication with a typed refusal and preserves queued writes for the rebuilt
device. Its reads are denied, rather than pretending it is an offline link.

Optimized wasm carries one `snapback4.engine` custom section with UTF-8
engine JSON. Native's stamp is returned by the binary, not a sidecar. `check`
and `doctor` inspect Exact apps mounted to this backend's generated operations
before UI testing. Discovery covers app.json in the backend root and its immediate
child app directories whose backend source mount resolves to this project's
generated operations. Custom declarations are read from app.json, app.ts and
mounted adapter entries (snapback.ts for directories, or the mounted source file).
Relative declarations resolve beside their source; /assets/ URLs resolve inside
the app after URL decoding and query/fragment removal. A declared `snapback4/ts` device adapter requires its default
wasm asset; relocated device adapters are recognized by their runtime web-device
imports and re-exports, including namespace/default and static dynamic forms.

### 20.12.1 Wave 14 development clock windows (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. The ordinary scheduler and default clock advance
keep latest-window coalescing: production must not replay an unbounded backlog
of effects after downtime. Development tests can request `clock advance

chronological boundary order, with declaration order breaking ties. Jobs drain
in that order on the final advanced clock; their periodStart/periodEnd arguments
name historical windows, while `now` remains the final advanced time. This is backlog admission, not
a historical simulation: test period-dependent work using periodStart/periodEnd,
not now. To test job logic dependent on now at each boundary, issue separate
advances. Scheduled
actions are admitted in order at the final advanced time (their deadlines
begin at admission, never at a historical periodEnd); external workers retain
their normal async execution contract. First-seen/changed schedules only prime their current
boundary. Held schedules remain held. Boundary/job admission keeps the existing
atomic saved-boundary transaction; restart never replays an admitted boundary.

Preflight always validates report metadata, including with held/replay schedules;
the hold only suppresses boundary enumeration/admission. Under --each-boundary it enumerates at most 10,000 crossed windows in total across all schedules, before
moving time or leases. A conservative report-size estimate also refuses above
15 MiB, reserving 1 MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host's
16 MiB JSON encoder (and the existing 64 MiB CLI HTTP exchange).

preflight includes the worst JSON escaping cost for that preview per schedule;
full error/args/cursor payloads are never loaded into the report.
Larger requests refuse E_CLOCK_INPUT and advise a smaller
advance; this bounds both execution and the explicit report. Each schedule's
reply includes firedWindows and skippedBoundaries (periodStart, periodEnd and,
for each skipped job window, its literal `snapback4 run <job>` command).
Coalesced advances have no crossed-window cap: they execute only the latest window
per schedule, retain the exact skippedPeriods count, and preview the first 32
skippedBoundaries per schedule with skippedBoundariesOmitted naming the remainder.
Report preflight budgets that bounded preview independently of jump distance.
Calendar schedules use calendar previous/next, never fixed-duration division.
An incomplete drain reports the already advanced now/skewMs and a refusal;
shutdown specifically refuses non-retryable E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED rather than
turning a stopped job/delivery loop into successful HTTP 200;

provides an explicit fresh-test recovery if the scenario should be restarted. The default retains
existing fired/periodStart/periodEnd fields and adds the explicit windows.

//! Bounded, read-only evidence from the store's own commit clock.
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

const SKIPPED_PREVIEW: usize = 32;
const TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 5_000;
const RECENT_COMMITS: usize = 64;
const TABLE_BYTES: usize = 4096;
const TABLE_NAMES: usize = 8;
const REPORT_ERROR_BYTES: usize = 1024;
// Reserve one MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host encoder.
const REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET: usize = crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING - (1 << 20);

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — inspect a fixed recent commit window, never application row values.
pub fn ahead(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
    // Reverse primary-key traversal: no full scan, sort, row payloads or retained
    // historical stamp set. octet_length(column) uses SQLite record metadata;
    // unlike a text length/cast/substr, it need not load oversized payloads.
    let mut query = engine.store().conn().prepare(
        "SELECT at, CASE WHEN at > ?1 AND octet_length(touched) <= ?2 THEN CAST(touched AS BLOB) END,
         octet_length(touched) FROM _commits ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT ?3")?;
    let mut rows = query.query(rusqlite::params![now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS), TABLE_BYTES as i64, RECENT_COMMITS as i64])?;
    let mut lead = 0;
    let mut tables = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut omitted = false;
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        let at: i64 = row.get(0)?;
        if at <= now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS) { continue; }
        lead = lead.max(at.saturating_sub(now));
        let length: i64 = row.get(2)?;
        if length > TABLE_BYTES as i64 { omitted = true; continue; }
        let bytes: Vec<u8> = row.get(1)?;
        match serde_json::from_slice::<Vec<String>>(&bytes) {
            Ok(names) => for name in names {
                if tables.contains(&name) { continue; }
                if tables.len() < TABLE_NAMES && name.len() <= 128 { tables.insert(name); }
                else { omitted = true; }
            },
            Err(_) => omitted = true,
        }
    }
    if lead == 0 { return Ok(None); }
    let mut names = tables.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if omitted { names.push("additional tables omitted".into()); }
    if names.is_empty() { names.push("no table names recorded".into()); }
    Ok(Some(format!(
        "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD: recent commit stamps lead the current clock by up to {lead}ms (latest {RECENT_COMMITS} commits; tables: {}); use a fresh store per offset test ({}), or restart with the same --clock-offset (or dev --resume-clock) to continue",
        names.join(", "), crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())))
}

/// UTC calendar text for Unix milliseconds: schedule boundaries are UTC.
pub fn utc(ms: i64) -> String {
    let (days, rem) = (ms.div_euclid(86_400_000), ms.rem_euclid(86_400_000));
    // Civil-from-days (proleptic Gregorian), exact for the I53 range.
    let z = days + 719_468;
    let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
    let doe = z - era * 146_097;
    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1_460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
    let day = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
    let month = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
    let year = yoe + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z", rem / 3_600_000, rem % 3_600_000 / 60_000, rem % 60_000 / 1_000)
}

/// A positive duration in the units `clock advance` accepts, rounded up to a second.
pub fn span(ms: i64) -> String {
    let seconds = (ms.max(0) + 999) / 1_000;
    let (d, h, m, s) = (seconds / 86_400, seconds % 86_400 / 3_600, seconds % 3_600 / 60, seconds % 60);
    let parts: Vec<String> = [(d, "d"), (h, "h"), (m, "m"), (s, "s")].into_iter().filter(|(n, _)| *n > 0).map(|(n, unit)| format!("{n}{unit}")).collect();
    if parts.is_empty() { "0s".into() } else { parts.join("") }
}

/// Each schedule's saved boundary, in declaration order.
pub fn saved_boundaries(engine: &Engine) -> Vec<Option<i64>> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter()
        .map(|schedule| {
            let shape = engine.store().meta(&format!("schedule-shape:{}", schedule.name)).ok().flatten();
            if shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|shape| *shape != snapback4_core::value::digest(schedule)) { return None; }
            engine.store().meta(&format!("schedule:{}", schedule.name)).ok().flatten().and_then(|value| value.parse().ok())
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Preflight is bounded before the clock or worker leases change.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — only explicit execution enumerates all crossed windows; coalesced reports keep a bounded preview.
pub fn crossed_boundaries(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool) -> Result<Vec<(usize, i64)>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    let mut boundaries = Vec::new();
    let mut report_bytes = 0usize;
    for (index, schedule) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate() {
        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add(2048 + 6 * (REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3) + 8 * (schedule.name.len() + schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
        if held { continue; }
        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
        let end = schedule.boundary(now);
        if old >= end { continue; }
        let periods = schedule.periods_between(old, end);
        if each_boundary && periods > (10_000 - boundaries.len()) as i64 {
            return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("--each-boundary crosses more than 10000 schedule windows; use a smaller duration or omit --each-boundary to coalesce"));
        }
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add((if each_boundary { periods as usize } else { (periods as usize).min(SKIPPED_PREVIEW + 1) }).saturating_mul(1024 + 8 * schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule window report exceeds its byte budget; use a smaller duration")); }
        if !each_boundary { continue; }
        let mut boundary = schedule.next(old);
        while boundary <= end {
            boundaries.push((index, boundary));
            boundary = schedule.next(boundary);
        }
    }
    boundaries.sort_by_key(|(index, at)| (*at, *index));
    Ok(boundaries)
}

fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
    schedule.describe()
}

/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
        let next = schedule.next(current);
        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
            entry["fired"] = true.into();
            let start = schedule.previous(end);
            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
            let mut windows = Vec::new();
            if each_boundary {
                if let Some(old) = old {
                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
                }
            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
            if each_boundary {
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
            }
            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
            if skipped > 0 {
                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
                let mut windows = Vec::new();
                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
                    let mut item = window(at);
                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
                    }
                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
                }
                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
            }
        } else if held {
            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held (dev --hold-schedules or --replay); a restart without the flag resumes at the next boundary".into();
        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > current) {
            let due = schedule.next(saved);
            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: its saved boundary {} is ahead of the clock {} (a dev restart resets the clock offset; the store keeps the boundary). Advance {} more, restart with dev --resume-clock, or test on dev --fresh",
                utc(saved), utc(now), span(due - now)).into();
        } else if old != saved {
            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        } else {
            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        }
        entry
    }).collect()
}

/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
    let mut statement = engine.store().conn().prepare(
        "SELECT name, state, rows_written, substr(error, 1, ?1), octet_length(error) > ?1, id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 64")?;
    let mut rows = statement.query([REPORT_ERROR_BYTES as i64])?;
    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
        let name: String = row.get(0)?;
        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
        let mut error: Option<String> = row.get(3)?;
        let truncated = row.get::<_, Option<bool>>(4)?.unwrap_or(false);
        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());
            while !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; }
            text.truncate(end); text.push('…');
        }
        summaries.insert(name, serde_json::json!({ "id":row.get::<_, String>(5)?, "state":row.get::<_, String>(1)?,
            "rowsWritten":row.get::<_, i64>(2)?, "error":error, "errorTruncated":truncated }));
    }
    Ok(summaries)
}

/// Startup lines for schedules the store holds: their saved boundary leads the clock.
pub fn held_schedules(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Vec<String> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(saved_boundaries(engine)).filter_map(|(schedule, saved)| {
        let saved = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > schedule.boundary(now))?;
        let due = schedule.next(saved);
        Some(format!("clock     schedule {} is held until {} ({} from now): its saved boundary {} is ahead of this clock; `clock advance {}` or restart with dev --resume-clock",
            schedule.name, utc(due), span(due - now), utc(saved), span(due - now)))
    }).collect()
}

/// The dev clock offset that continues a store's own time: its newest
/// recent commit stamp (or saved schedule boundary) less wall time, never negative.
pub fn resume_offset(store: &snapback4_core::Store, wall: i64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    let commit: Option<i64> = store.conn().query_row(
        "SELECT MAX(at) FROM (SELECT at FROM _commits ORDER BY seq DESC LIMIT ?1)", [RECENT_COMMITS as i64], |row| row.get(0))?;
    let mut statement = store.conn().prepare("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'schedule:%'")?;
    let boundary = statement.query_map([], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0))?.flatten().filter_map(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).max();
    let latest = commit.into_iter().chain(boundary).max().unwrap_or(wall);
    // Whole seconds, rounded up, so restarting twice does not drift backwards.
    Ok((latest.saturating_sub(wall).max(0) + 999) / 1_000 * 1_000)
}

pub fn has_data(engine: &Engine) -> bool {
    engine.store().conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r)", [], |r| r.get(0)).unwrap_or(false)
}

pub fn offset_line(offset: i64, running: bool) -> String {
    format!("clock     offset from wall time: {offset:+}ms{}", if running { " (lasts until restart)" } else { " (no running dev; inspecting against wall time)" })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn engine() -> Engine {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table alpha:\n  at: time\n  public\ntable beta:\n  at: time\n  public\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program), 100_000).unwrap()
    }
    fn seed(engine: &mut Engine, table: &str, commit_at: i64, row_at: i64) {
        engine.seed_at([(table.into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
            ("id", snapback4_core::Value::Id(snapback4_core::ids::mint(row_at, &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).unwrap())),
            ("at", snapback4_core::Value::Int(row_at)),
        ]))], commit_at).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_bounds_one_large_job_error_repeated_across_many_schedules() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..500 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        assert_eq!(crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap().len(), 500);
        let fired = engine.fire_schedules(60_000).unwrap();
        let message = "🧪\n\\".repeat(30_000);
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE _jobs SET error = ?1 WHERE id = (SELECT id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1)", [&message]).unwrap();
        let report = schedule_report(&engine, &before, &fired, 60_000, false, false, None);
        assert_eq!(report.len(), 500);
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["errorTruncated"] == true));
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["error"].as_str().unwrap().len() <= REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3));
        assert!(serde_json::to_vec(&report).unwrap().len() < 2 << 20);
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_refuses_before_the_host_encoder_limit() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..3000 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        let refusal = crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_CLOCK_INPUT");
        assert!(refusal.message.contains("byte budget"));
        assert_eq!(before, saved_boundaries(&engine));
        assert!(REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET < crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING);
    }
    #[test]
    fn utc_text_and_spans_match_the_calendar() {
        assert_eq!(utc(0), "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(951_782_400_000), "2000-02-29T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(-1), "1969-12-31T23:59:59Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(1_791_468_000_000), "2026-10-08T14:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(span(12_633_000), "3h30m33s");
        assert_eq!(span(86_400_001), "1d1s");
        assert_eq!(span(0), "0s");
    }
    #[test]
    fn resume_offset_follows_the_newest_commit_and_is_never_negative() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 0);
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 3_700_500, 1);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 3_601_000);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 9_000_000).unwrap(), 0);
    }
    #[test]
    fn commit_tolerance_and_largest_lead_ignore_future_application_values() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 100_000, 1_000_000);
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 105_000, 2_000_000);
        seed(&mut engine, "beta", 107_000, 3_000_000);
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7000ms (latest 64 commits; tables: beta)"), "{note}");
        let note = ahead(&engine, 99_999).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7001ms (latest 64 commits; tables: alpha, beta)"), "{note}");
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 102_000).unwrap().is_none());
    }
    #[test]
    fn recent_window_does_not_claim_older_commits_or_read_row_payloads() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 200_000, 200_000);
        for n in 0..RECENT_COMMITS { seed(&mut engine, "beta", 100_000, 300_000 + n as i64); }
        // Deliberately unreadable images: metadata inspection must not deserialize them.
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data = 'not json'", []).unwrap();
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE _commits SET at = 200000, touched = ?1 WHERE seq = (SELECT MAX(seq) FROM _commits)", ["x".repeat(TABLE_BYTES + 1)]).unwrap();
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("100000ms") && note.contains("additional tables omitted"), "{note}");
    }
}
