fn seed_refusal(error: snapback4_core::RuntimeError) -> Refusal {
    let mut refusal = error.refusal();
    // Reseeding with changed immutable values reads like a rule problem; say what happened.
    if refusal.code == "E_CONSTRAINT" && refusal.message.ends_with(" is immutable") {
        refusal.message.push_str("; /seed is an upsert by id, and a row with that id already exists with a different value in that column. Reseed it with its original value, give the row a new id, POST /dev/reset first, or restart dev with --memory for an empty store");
    }
    refusal
}

/// What `POST /dev/reset` was asked to do: the body is empty, a `/seed`
/// array, or `{"seed": [..], "clock": "reset"}` with either key optional.
#[derive(Debug, Default, PartialEq)]
struct ResetRequest { seed: Vec<Json>, clock: bool }

fn reset_request(body: &str) -> Result<ResetRequest, Refusal> {
    const SHAPE: &str = "reset takes no body, a /seed array [{table, row}, ..], or {\"seed\": [..], \"clock\": \"reset\"} with either key optional";
    if body.trim().is_empty() { return Ok(ResetRequest::default()); }
    match serde_json::from_str(body) {
        Ok(Json::Array(seed)) => Ok(ResetRequest { seed, clock: false }),
        Ok(Json::Object(fields)) => {
            let mut request = ResetRequest::default();
            for (key, value) in fields {
                match (key.as_str(), value) {
                    ("seed", Json::Array(seed)) => request.seed = seed,
                    ("clock", Json::String(clock)) if clock == "reset" => request.clock = true,
                    ("clock", Json::String(clock)) if clock == "keep" => request.clock = false,
                    ("clock", other) => return Err(crate::dev_clock::input(format!("reset's clock is \"reset\" (back to wall time) or \"keep\" (the default), not {other}"))),
                    (key, _) => return Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{SHAPE}; got key {key:?}"))),
                }
            }
            Ok(request)
        }
        _ => Err(Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", SHAPE)),
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.25 — one transaction: empty, new identity, reseed; then the host's caches follow.
pub(super) fn reset(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    if !shared.personas || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&reset_release()) }));
        return;
    }
    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("testing")) }));
    let asked = match reset_request(body) {
        Ok(asked) => asked,
        Err(refusal) => return refuse(request, refusal),
    };
    // Serialize with clock advances, then take the engine before the clock:
    // the order clock advance and worker claims use.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock).clone();
    if asked.clock && let Err(refusal) = clock.reset() { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
    let now = clock.now();
    // Resolve against the prospective reset clock before changing any durable state.
    let typed = match typed_rows(engine.schema(), &asked.seed, now) {
        Ok(typed) => typed,
        Err(refusal) => { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
    };
    let reset = match engine.dev_reset_at(typed, now) {
        Ok(reset) => reset,
        Err(error) => {
            drop(engine);
            return respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) }));
        }
    };
    if asked.clock { *lock(&shared.clock) = clock; }
    *lock(&shared.dev_reset_store) = Some(reset.store_id.clone());
    // The reset committed a new identity and a new authority clock. Replace,
    // rather than max with, the retired store's cached high-water mark.
    shared.time_audience_clock.store(if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|t| t.not_before.is_some()) { now } else { i64::MIN }, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Release);
    // Detached readers reopen; the following registry, change answers and
    // `/dev/status` forget the old identity. Wake then names the new store,
    // so every held poll returns and each client sees the replacement.
    let sites = lock(&shared.bound).refusal_sites.clone();
    lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &sites);
    lock(&shared.adoption).store_id = reset.store_id.clone();
    if let Ok(reader) = snapback4_core::engine::following_history::Reader::new() { *lock(&shared.following) = reader; }
    *lock(&shared.change_answers) = Default::default();
    shared.durable_committed(&engine);
    let generation = engine.generation();
    drop(engine);
    let assets = reset.assets.len();
    if let Err(error) = reset.assets.remove_files() { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: reset left asset bytes behind: {error}"); }
    let now = shared.now();
    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: store reset (store {} → {}, seeded {}) in {} ms", reset.previous_store_id, reset.store_id, reset.seeded, started.elapsed().as_millis());
    respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({
        "reset": true, "seeded": reset.seeded, "generation": generation, "storeId": reset.store_id,
        "previousStoreId": reset.previous_store_id, "head": reset.seq, "assetsRemoved": assets,
        "now": now, "clockOffsetMs": lock(&shared.clock).offset(), "skewMs": now - snapback4_core::now_ms(), "ms": started.elapsed().as_millis() as u64,
    }));
}

pub(super) fn reset_release() -> Refusal {
    Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "resetting the store is a development door; snapback4 serve never empties a store")
        .guide("testing")
}

// Device time is an estimate, never an input to server authorization or settlement.
import type { DeviceCore } from "./local.ts";

const WINDOW_MS = 60_000;
const MAX_SAMPLES = 32;
const SHORT_RTT_MS = 1000;
const clocks = new WeakMap<typeof fetch, ServerClock>();
const readers = new WeakMap<() => number, ServerClock>();
const responses = new WeakMap<Response, { clock: ServerClock; order: number }>();
interface Sample { offset: number; roundTrip: number; received: number; provisional: boolean }

/** One bounded, minimum-round-trip window, reset on a server clock revision.
 * A clock advance must not compete with faster samples from before the jump. */
export class ServerClock {
  private offset = 0;
  private issuedOffset: number | undefined;
  private last = 0;
  private samples: Sample[] = [];
  private revision: string | undefined;
  private order = 0;
  private epochBarrier = 0;
  private listeners = new Set<(shift: number, reschedule: boolean) => void>();
  private save: ((value: string) => Promise<void>) | undefined;
  private saving: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
  private lastSave: { owner: (value: string) => Promise<void>; value: string; result: Promise<void> } | undefined;
  constructor(private wall: () => number = Date.now) { readers.set(this.issuedDeviceNow, this); }
  fork(): ServerClock {
    const copy = new ServerClock(this.wall);
    copy.offset = this.offset; copy.issuedOffset = this.issuedOffset; copy.last = this.last;
    copy.samples = this.samples.slice(); copy.revision = this.revision;
    copy.order = this.order; copy.epochBarrier = this.epochBarrier;
    return copy;
  }
  /** Unfloored estimated server time: samples and server-answer refetches. */
  estimatedServerNow = (): number => Math.min(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, Math.max(0, Math.round(this.wall() + this.offset)));
  private deviceCandidate = (): number => Math.min(Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER, Math.max(0, Math.round(this.wall() + (this.issuedOffset ?? 0))));
  /** Monotonic issued device time: execution, prediction, expiry and rendering. */
  issuedDeviceNow = (): number => this.last = Math.max(this.last, this.deviceCandidate());
  /** Adopt a committed admission without changing the server-time estimate. */
  raiseIssued(time: number): void {
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(time) || time < 0 || time <= this.last) return;
    const shift = time - Math.max(this.last, this.deviceCandidate());
    this.last = time;
    // Even a one-millisecond floor increase may cross a frozen deadline.
    if (shift > 0) for (const listener of this.listeners) listener(shift, true);
  }
  subscribe = (listener: (shift: number, reschedule: boolean) => void): (() => void) => {
    this.listeners.add(listener);
    return () => { this.listeners.delete(listener); };
  };
  sample(serverNow: number, sent: number, received: number, revision?: string, order = ++this.order, provisional = false) {
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(serverNow) || serverNow < 0 || !Number.isFinite(sent) || !Number.isFinite(received) || received < sent) return;
    this.order = Math.max(this.order, order);
    const revisionChanged = revision !== undefined && revision !== this.revision;
    if (revisionChanged) {
      // A delayed reply from before an advance cannot restore the old window.
      const split = (value: string) => value.split(":");
      const [epoch, version] = split(revision), [priorEpoch, priorVersion] = split(this.revision ?? "");
      if (epoch === priorEpoch ? Number(version) < Number(priorVersion) : this.revision !== undefined && order <= this.epochBarrier) return;
      this.samples = [];
      this.revision = revision;
      // Every request already issued may still have reached the old process.
      if (epoch !== priorEpoch) this.epochBarrier = this.order;
    }
    const before = this.offset, issuedBefore = this.issuedOffset;
    const roundTrip = received - sent;
    // Long responses use a conservative receipt date, never a midpoint that
    // could latch half a server wait into the monotonic floor.
    provisional ||= roundTrip > SHORT_RTT_MS;
    this.samples = this.samples.filter(s => received >= s.received && received - s.received <= WINDOW_MS).slice(-(MAX_SAMPLES - 1));
    this.samples.push({ offset: serverNow - (provisional ? received : (sent + received) / 2), roundTrip, received, provisional });
    // Every short measured sample outranks provisional receipt stamps.
    const best = this.samples.reduce((best, s) => s.provisional !== best.provisional ? (best.provisional ? s : best) : s.roundTrip <= best.roundTrip ? s : best);
    this.offset = best.offset;
    this.issuedOffset = best.offset;
    if (before !== this.offset || issuedBefore !== this.issuedOffset) {
      void this.persist().catch(() => {}); // Device calls/close await the same durable save.
      // Watchers ignore sub-frame noise. A newly eligible issued offset can
      // move even when a short sample merely confirms the server estimate.
      const estimateShift = this.offset - before, issuedShift = (this.issuedOffset ?? 0) - (issuedBefore ?? 0);
      const shift = Math.abs(issuedShift) > Math.abs(estimateShift) ? issuedShift : estimateShift;
      for (const listener of this.listeners) listener(shift, revisionChanged);
    }
  }
  wrap(fetcher: typeof fetch): typeof fetch {
    if (clocks.get(fetcher) === this) return fetcher;
    const wrapped: typeof fetch = async (input, init) => {
      const sent = this.wall(), order = ++this.order;
      const response = await fetcher(input, init);
      const received = this.wall();
      responses.set(response, { clock: this, order });
      const stamp = response.headers.get("x-snapback-time");
      if (stamp !== null) this.sample(Number(stamp), sent, received, response.headers.get("x-snapback-clock") ?? undefined, order);
      return response;
    };
    clocks.set(wrapped, this);
    return wrapped;
  }
  serverDelay(deadline: number): number { return Math.max(0, deadline - this.estimatedServerNow()); }
  deviceDelay(deadline: number): number {
    return this.issuedDeviceNow() >= deadline ? 0 : Math.max(0, deadline - this.deviceCandidate());
  }
  sampleStream(serverNow: number, revision: string | undefined, order: number) {
    const received = this.wall();
    // The server writes this stamp at the end of its hold, not at request send.
    this.sample(serverNow, received, received, revision, order, true);
  }
  async attach(core: DeviceCore) {
    const kept = await core.call({ op: "meta", key: "server-clock" });
    if (kept.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(kept.denied));
    try {
      const state = JSON.parse(String(kept.ok)) as { offset: number; issuedOffset?: number } | null;
      const issuedOffset = state?.issuedOffset ?? state?.offset;
      if (state && Number.isFinite(state.offset) && Math.abs(state.offset) <= Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER && typeof issuedOffset === "number" && Number.isFinite(issuedOffset) && Math.abs(issuedOffset) <= Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER) {
        if (!this.samples.length) this.offset = state.offset;
        this.issuedOffset ??= issuedOffset;
      }
    } catch { /* Missing or malformed clock metadata starts at offset zero. */ }
    // Only admissions restore a durable floor. Old offset metadata's `last`
    // may be a wall-time now() call and must not override a startup correction.
    // The device commits this floor atomically with admission, and retains it
    // after settlement. Pending entries also recover older stores/journals.
    // Backfill legacy admission times transactionally before replay can retire
    // the queue; offset persistence deliberately cannot retain an issued floor.
    const pending = await core.call({ op: "queued", restore_clock_floor: true });
    if (pending.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(pending.denied));
    const floor = await core.call({ op: "meta", key: "server-clock-floor" });
    if (floor.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(floor.denied));
    const issued = Number(floor.ok);
    if (Number.isSafeInteger(issued) && issued >= 0) this.last = Math.max(this.last, issued);
    for (const entry of (pending.ok ?? []) as { now?: number }[]) {
      if (Number.isSafeInteger(entry.now) && entry.now! >= 0) this.last = Math.max(this.last, entry.now!);
    }
    this.save = async value => {
      const reply = await core.call({ op: "set_meta", key: "server-clock", value });
      if (reply.denied) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(reply.denied));
    };
    await this.persist();
  }
  // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.3 — share unchanged offset persistence, never admission floors.
  persist(): Promise<void> {
    if (!this.save) return Promise.resolve();
    const save = this.save, value = JSON.stringify({ offset: this.offset, issuedOffset: this.issuedOffset ?? 0 });
    if (this.lastSave?.owner === save && this.lastSave.value === value) return this.lastSave.result;
    this.saving = this.saving.catch(() => {}).then(() => save(value));
    const saved = this.lastSave = { owner: save, value, result: this.saving };
    // A failed save remains a failure to its callers, and is retried by the
    // next caller. Attaching another device always supplies a new owner.
    void this.saving.catch(() => { if (this.lastSave === saved) this.lastSave = undefined; });
    return this.saving;
  }
  async detach() { const pending = this.persist(); this.save = undefined; await pending; }
}

export const serverClock = (fetcher: typeof fetch): ServerClock => clocks.get(fetcher) ?? new ServerClock();

/** Streaming final stamps are provisional receipt-time samples, never poll midpoints. */
export function sampleChanges(response: Response, body: { serverNow?: number; clockRevision?: string }) {
  const sample = responses.get(response);
  if (sample && body.serverNow !== undefined) sample.clock.sampleStream(body.serverNow, body.clockRevision, sample.order);
}

export function onClock(now: () => number, listener: () => void): () => void {
  return readers.get(now)?.subscribe(listener) ?? (() => {});
}

export function clockDelay(now: () => number, deadline: number): number {
  return readers.get(now)?.deviceDelay(deadline) ?? Math.max(0, deadline - now());

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#10 — packed starters must pass their real web toolchains
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { createServer } from "node:net";
import { Worker } from "node:worker_threads";
import { loadavg } from "node:os";
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { mkdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, existsSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve, join } from "node:path";

// The live keeper alone signals its own group. Closing its private pipe also
// cleans up if this runner dies; no cached PID ever authorizes a signal.
export async function runToolchainCommand(command, args, { cwd, env, timeout }) {
  const owner = fileURLToPath(new URL("./test-command-owner.mjs", import.meta.url));
  const keeper = spawn(process.execPath, [owner, "100", command, ...args], { cwd, env, detached: true, stdio: ["pipe", "inherit", "inherit", "ipc"] });
  let released = false, completed = false, failure, status;
  const release = mode => { if (!released) { released = true; keeper.stdin.end(mode); } };
  const fail = error => { failure ??= error; release("TERM"); };
  const timer = setTimeout(() => fail(Error(`${command}: toolchain command timed out`)), timeout);
  keeper.stdin.on("error", error => { failure ??= error; });
  keeper.on("message", message => {
    if (message.error) fail(Error(message.error));
    if (message.exited) { completed = true; status = message.status; release("KILL"); }
  });
  await new Promise(done => {
    keeper.once("error", error => { failure ??= error; });
    keeper.once("close", done);
  });
  clearTimeout(timer);
  if (!released || !completed) failure ??= Error(`${command}: command owner exited without a result`);
  // This read-only group-existence probe needs no /proc mount in the clean
  // userspace. Refuse on lingering members or ambiguous/permission errors.
  // Two empty observations also protect against a transient inspection result.
  const deadline = performance.now() + 5_000;
  let empty = 0;
  if (keeper.pid) while (empty < 2) {
    try { process.kill(-keeper.pid, 0); empty = 0; }
    catch (error) { if (error.code === "ESRCH") empty++; else { failure ??= error; break; } }
    if (empty === 2) break;
    if (performance.now() >= deadline) { failure ??= Error(`${command}: toolchain process group did not drain`); break; }
    await sleep(50);
  }
  if (failure) throw failure;
  if (status !== 0) throw Error(`${command}: toolchain command exited ${status}`);
}

  { id: "expo-social", kind: "expo", template: "social", flags: ["--template", "social"] },
];
async function boundedStop(work, label) {
  let timer;
  try { return await Promise.race([work, new Promise((_, reject) => { timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(label + " timed out")), 10_000); })]); }
  finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
import { hangBudget, waitFor } from '../packages/snapback4/test/helpers/wait.mjs';
// What every Chromium witness needs: the local-first client bundled into a
// page, a seeded `snapback4 dev`, and a browser with a CDP session for the
// heap. Each witness seeds its own rows and measures its own thing.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { mkdtempSync, cpSync, rmSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, globSync, existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from "node:url";

export const root = resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

/** The binary under test: `SNAPBACK4_BIN` (a release build for throughput numbers), else the debug build. */
export const binary = () => process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN ? resolve(process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN) : join(root, "target/debug/snapback4");

export function chromiumExecutable() {
  const candidates = [
    ...globSync(`${process.env.HOME}/Library/Caches/ms-playwright/chromium-*/chrome-mac-arm64/Google Chrome for Testing.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome for Testing`).sort().reverse(),
    ...globSync(`${process.env.HOME}/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-*/chrome-mac-arm64/Google Chrome for Testing.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome for Testing`).sort().reverse(),
    ...globSync(`${process.env.HOME}/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-*/chrome-linux64/chrome`).sort().reverse(),
    ...globSync(`${process.env.HOME}/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-*/chrome-linux/chrome`).sort().reverse(),
    "/usr/bin/google-chrome",
    "/usr/bin/chromium",
    "/usr/bin/chromium-browser",
    "/Applications/Google Chrome.app/Contents/MacOS/Google Chrome",
    "/Applications/Chromium.app/Contents/MacOS/Chromium",
  ];
  const exe = candidates.find((path) => existsSync(path));
  if (!exe) throw new Error("no Chromium: install playwright's chromium or Google Chrome");
  return exe;
}

/** The chat backend's scale seed: `groups` conversations of `messages`
 * each, `members` principals in every one (alice admin, bob member, then
 * fillers), so bob's partition is the whole thing. */
export function scaleSeed({ groups, messages, members }) {
  const rows = [];
  const fillers = Array.from({ length: Math.max(0, members - 2) }, (_, n) => `dev:member${n}`);

    const conversation = `c${String(g).padStart(3, "0")}`;
    rows.push({ table: "conversations", row: { id: conversation, title: `Group ${g}`, creatorId: "dev:alice" } });
    for (const [n, principal] of ["dev:alice", "dev:bob", ...fillers].entries()) {

 and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits

+
+## 12. Wave 15 proof diagnostics and clock sampling (KIT4)
+
+Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-18, commissioned for 0.4.32. On a canonical
+argument mismatch the shared E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED message names the differing
+top-level keys. withBrowser installs a diagnostic binding which writes the
+full armed and actual arguments to a fresh mode-0600 *.witness.private.jsonl
+file under the proof log directory. Receipt messages contain no argument values.
+Diagnostic failure never cancels, retries or blocks the application's write.
+This supersedes §11's suppression of argument values in all retained logs;
+private proof logs intentionally contain sensitive application evidence.
+
+Substring timeout advice requires an explicitly scoped renderedText or
+waitForFresh locator and a substring within that scope. Whole-page prose
+cannot trigger the hint. A timeout includes up to three visible page headings
+(300 characters total); it does not weaken exact matching or ambiguity errors.
+
+waitForClock(page, advancedNow, {timeoutMs=30000}) polls the registered
+witness's signed-in client.now(), returning its number once it reaches the
+advance reply's now. It never sets page/driver time or fetches through a
+separate clock. Missing witness/auth, invalid time and the bounded timeout
+refuse explicitly. This is clock observation, not proof of business settlement.
+
+loadFixtures validates options.url before URL parsing and names the rejected
+persona or SHA-256 credential fingerprint without printing bearer bytes.
+startStatic names build:web or build from the project package scripts when
+its output directory is absent. withBrowser resolves playwright-core first,
+with an actionable missing-peer refusal before creating logs or launching a
+browser; the full day-two recipe enters withBrowser before build/deploy.
+
+The source-generated proof API table includes every runtime and type export,
+signatures, result fields and @throws reasons. The packed qualification executes
+the literal wire blocks and production-account day-two script unchanged, plus
+a 30-day two-schedule coalesced advance, explicit cap refusal, memory-host replay
+and array alias case. The production starter's web select labels use explicit
+htmlFor/id pairs. Production entry/auth UI behavior remains with its owning
+production workstream; this amendment changes those label associations only.
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

## Moves

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
    };
    let reply=second.auth_request("POST",&path,Value::Null,&format!("x-snapback-dev-instance: {}",old["instance"].as_str().unwrap()));
    assert_eq!(reply["denied"]["code"],"E_INPUT","{reply}");
    assert_eq!(second.request("GET","/dev/latency",Value::Null),before);
    let cleared=output_json(f.command(&["doctor","--latency","--reset","--json"]));
    assert_eq!(cleared["operations"]["/q/time"]["n"],0);
}

#[test]
fn kit3_clock_reports_skipped_windows_and_each_boundary_preserves_order() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-windows");
    let source = "use personas alice\ntable windows:\n  start: time\n  end: time\n  at: time\n  public 'test'\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ntable drivers:\n  n: int\n  cap 1\n  public 'test'\n  online only\nquery windows():\n  return windows first 100 by .id\njob record(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):\n  for d in drivers:\n    insert windows {start: periodStart, end: periodEnd, at: now}\nschedule minute every 1m: record\nschedule two every 2m: record\n";
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), source).unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let port = host.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap();
    host.post("/seed", json!([{"table":"drivers","row":{"id":"one","n":0}}]));
    let advance = output_json(f.command(&["clock", "advance", "3m", "--port", port]));
    assert!(advance["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap() >= 180_000 - 100, "{advance}");
    let minute = advance["schedules"].as_array().unwrap().iter().find(|s| s["name"] == "minute").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(minute["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(minute["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 2);
    for skipped in minute["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap() {
        assert_eq!(skipped["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap() - skipped["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap(), 60_000);
        assert!(skipped["command"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with("snapback4 run record '{"));
    }
    let reset = host.post("/dev/reset", json!({"clock":"reset","seed":[{"table":"drivers","row":{"id":"one","n":0}}]}));
    assert!(reset["now"].is_i64() && reset["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs() < 1000, "{reset}");
    let advance = output_json(f.command(&["clock", "advance", "3m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]));
    let mut expected = Vec::new();
    for schedule in advance["schedules"].as_array().unwrap() {
        assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundaries"], json!([]));
        for window in schedule["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap() { expected.push(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()); }
    }
    expected.sort();
    assert!(expected.len() >= 4);
    let rows = host.query("windows");
    let rows = rows.as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(rows.iter().map(|r| r["end"].as_i64().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), expected);
    assert!(rows.iter().all(|r| r["at"].as_i64().unwrap() >= advance["now"].as_i64().unwrap()));
    let status = host.request("GET", "/dev/clock/status", json!({}));
    assert!(status["now"].is_i64() && status["skewMs"].is_i64(), "{status}");
    let before = host.query("windows");
    let refused = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "10001m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]);
    assert!(!refused.status.success());
    assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&refused.stderr).contains("10000 schedule windows"));
    assert_eq!(host.query("windows"), before, "preflight refuses before admitting any window");
}

#[test]
fn kit3_first_http_query_materializes_only_the_live_dependencies_it_encounters() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-opening");
    let source = "use personas alice\ntable announcements:\n  opens: time\n  until: time ?\n  note: text <=40\n  not before .opens\n  expire at .until\n  public 'test'\n  online only\nquery all():\n  return announcements first 100 by .id\nquery clock():\n  return now\n";
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), source).unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch", "--hold-schedules"]);
    let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();
    let mut seed = Vec::new();
    for n in 0..20 { seed.push(json!({"table":"announcements","row":{"id":format!("row{n:02}"),"opens":now+1000,"until":null,"note":"revealed"}})); }
    seed.push(json!({"table":"announcements","row":{"id":"never","opens":now+1000,"until":now+500,"note":"must stay private"}}));
    let seeded = host.post("/seed", json!(seed)); assert!(seeded.get("denied").is_none(), "{seeded}");
    host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"2s"}));
    let reply = host.post("/q/all", json!({"args":{}}));
    assert_eq!(reply["complete"], true, "{reply}");
    assert_eq!(reply["data"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 20);
    assert!(reply.get("loading").is_none() && reply.get("pending_opening").is_none(), "{reply}");
    assert!(!reply.to_string().contains("must stay private"));
}

#[test]
fn kit3_each_boundary_action_deadlines_start_at_admission() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-actions");
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use server\nuse personas alice\naction ping(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time) deadline 1m <- viewer = 'service:clock'\nschedule pulse every 1m as service:clock: ping\n").unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let advanced = host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"5m","eachBoundary":true}));
    assert_eq!(advanced["schedules"][0]["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 5, "{advanced}");
    let store = rusqlite::Connection::open(f.0.join(".snapback4/store.sqlite")).unwrap();
    let (count, failed, earliest): (i64, i64, i64) = store.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*), SUM(state = 'failed'), MIN(deadline) FROM _requests", [], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?, row.get(2)?))).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(count, 5); assert_eq!(failed, 0);
    assert!(earliest >= advanced["now"].as_i64().unwrap() + 60_000);
}

#[test]
fn kit3_large_clock_report_uses_complete_http_and_runnable_no_argument_commands() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-large-report");
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let port = host.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap();
    let output = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "2000m", "--port", port]);
    assert!(output.status.success());
    let reply = output_json(output);
    assert_eq!(reply["advancedMs"], 120_000_000);
    let skipped = reply["schedules"][0]["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 32);
    assert_eq!(reply["schedules"][0]["skippedBoundariesOmitted"], 967);
    let large = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "4000m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]);
    assert!(large.stdout.len() > 65_536, "explicit windows exercise the former transport ceiling");
    assert_eq!(output_json(large)["schedules"][0]["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 2000);
    assert_eq!(skipped[0]["command"], format!("snapback4 run tick '{{}}' --port {port}"));
    let replay = f.command(&["run", "tick", "{}"]);
    assert!(replay.status.success(), "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&replay.stderr));
}

#[test]
fn kit3_http_transitive_opening_dependencies_ignore_never_live_worlds() {
    let mut paired = Vec::new();
    for never_live in [false, true] {
        let f = Fixture::new(if never_live {"kit3-transitive-never"} else {"kit3-transitive-absent"});
        fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use personas alice\ntable grants:\n  ticket: int\n  opens: time\n  until: time ?\n  unique ticket\n  not before .opens\n  expire at .until\n  public 'scheduled grant'\n  online only\ntable tasks:\n  ticket: int\n  read <- exists grants[ticket = .ticket]\n  online only\nquery all():\n  return tasks first 100 by .id\nquery clock():\n  return now\n").unwrap();
        let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
        let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();
        let mut seed = Vec::new();
        for n in 0..20 {
            seed.push(json!({"table":"grants","row":{"id":format!("g{n:02}"),"ticket":n,"opens":now+1000,"until":null}}));
            seed.push(json!({"table":"tasks","row":{"id":format!("t{n:02}"),"ticket":n}}));
        }
        if never_live { seed.push(json!({"table":"grants","row":{"id":"secret-never","ticket":99,"opens":now+1000,"until":now+500}})); }
        let seeded = host.post("/seed", json!(seed)); assert!(seeded.get("denied").is_none(), "{seeded}");
        host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"2s"}));
        let reply = host.post("/q/all", json!({"args":{}}));
        assert!(reply.get("loading").is_none() && reply.get("denied").is_none(), "{reply}");
        assert_eq!(reply["data"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 20);
        paired.push(json!({"data":reply["data"],"complete":reply["complete"],"meter":reply["meter"]}));
    }
    assert_eq!(paired[0], paired[1], "never-live occupants change neither answer nor query meter");
}

#[test]
fn kit3_retained_search_overlap_waits_for_the_same_pending_publication() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-retained-opening");
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use personas alice\ntable members:\n  room: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique room, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  sync to .principal\ntable notes:\n  room: id\n  opens: time\n  at: time\n  body: text <=100\n  search body\n  by byOpening: opens\n  not before .opens\n  read <- exists members[.room, viewer]\n  sync to members[.room] last 100 by .at\n  retain delivered-history\nquery search(q: text <=100, c: cursor ?):\n  return notes matching q first 10 by .at after c\nquery clock():\n  return now\n").unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();
    let mut rows: Vec<_> = (0..8).map(|n| json!({"table":"notes","row":{"id":format!("n{n}"),"room":"r","at":n,"opens":now+1000,"body":"coffee"}})).collect();
    rows.push(json!({"table":"members","row":{"id":"member","room":"r","principal":"dev:alice"}}));
    let seeded = host.post("/seed", json!(rows)); assert!(seeded.get("denied").is_none(), "{seeded}");
    host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"2s"}));
    let reply = host.post("/q/search", json!({"args":{"q":"coffee","c":null},"searchRetained":["n7"]}));
    assert!(reply.get("denied").is_none() && reply.get("loading").is_none(), "{reply}");
    assert_eq!(reply["data"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 8, "{reply}");
    assert_eq!(reply["searchCurrent"], json!(["n7"]));
}

#[test]
fn kit3_held_schedules_still_preflight_report_size_without_releasing_the_hold() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-held-report");
    let mut source = SOURCE.replace("schedule ticking every 2m: tick\n", "");
    for n in 0..4200 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule held{n} every 2m: tick\n")); }
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), source).unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch", "--hold-schedules"]);
    let now = host.query("time").as_i64().unwrap();
    let created = host.post("/m/counter", json!({"args":{}})); assert_eq!(created["state"], "sent");
    let refused = host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"1h","eachBoundary":true}));
    assert_eq!(refused["denied"]["code"], "E_CLOCK_INPUT", "{refused}");
    assert!(refused["denied"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().contains("byte budget"));
    assert!(host.query("time").as_i64().unwrap() < now + 60_000, "failed preflight must not advance time");
    assert_eq!(host.query("counters")[0]["n"], 0, "the hold and queued state stay unchanged");
}

# Wire: HTTP for curl, scripts and non-JS clients

Every call carries a principal; public visitors use guest sessions; `serve` accepts bearers only. Plain JSON over HTTP works with curl, scripts and non-JS clients.

```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s -X POST $U/q/mine -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{}}'
curl -s -X POST $U/m/add -H 'x-snapback-persona: alice' -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"body":"hi"}}'
```

`snapback4 dev` serves on that address (choose another with `--port N`, or
`--port 0` for a free one; the first stdout line is the URL). A taken port
refuses the start at once with `E_ADDRINUSE`, naming the holder's pid. The JS client (`snapback4/client`) stays the
paved surface for apps: it adds sync, offline writes and live queries.

## Identity

/auth/config includes development true on dev and false on serve; show persona controls only in development.

In development, `x-snapback-persona: alice` acts as `dev:alice`: same machine
only, `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, and only for a name `use personas` declares; any other
name is 401 E_AUTH naming the declaration (a schema without `use personas`
admits any name). `snapback4 serve` refuses personas. Everywhere else send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, from
`/auth/guest` (`{}`), `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login` (`{"email","password"}`),
each answering `{"session":{principal,kind,token,expiresAt}}`; a worker's token
comes from `snapback4 token --service <name>`. Send one credential, not both.

## Wire and client envelopes

| Call | Success | Refusal / pending |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /m/<op>` | `{state:"sent", id?, seq, result, generation, store_id, head}`; inserted row: `result.id` | `{state:"failed", id?, why, current?, store_id, head}`; decode/auth/unknown-op failures can be `{denied}` |
| `POST /q/<op>` | `{data, complete, cut, next, seq, tables, viewer, generation, store_id}`; list, row, shape or scalar | `{denied, complete:false, …}` or `{loading:true,data:null,complete:false,…}`; loading data is a placeholder, never an empty answer |
| `POST /assets` raw bytes | `{asset:{id,type,bytes,width?,height?,duration?,name?}}`; `?as=file&name=…` for files | `{denied}`; pass `{id:asset.id}` to the mutation |
| `await client.query(api.op, args)` | `Read<T>`: `{data, existence, complete, …}`; narrow with `isReady` | `{denied}`; one-shot reads settle or refuse; observers can also load |
| `await client.mutate(api.op, args)` | `Write<T>`: `{state:"sent", id, seq, result?, …}` | `{state:"failed", id, why, …}` or `{state:"pending", id, …}`; pending is not success |

SDK envelopes differ from raw HTTP. Check `loading` and `denied` before `data`; HTTP status alone is insufficient.
An omitted mutation request id is omitted from its reply, never the inserted
row id. Send an id once per intended write to retry safely; SDK clients mint
it. Replays omit historical result/current; see the Operations contract below.

## Literal query and mutation replies

The fixture and complete replies below are generated by a real dev run in
qualification, using the installed package's binary. Only minted row/store ids
are normalized to row1/store1. All six calls return HTTP 200: inspect the body.
`list` as alice succeeds; the same query as bob refuses under `denied`.
`add` succeeds with title Proof, and refuses title refuse under `state`/`why`.
A point lookup (`one`) returns one object or null, never a one-element list.
A view scan (`countRows`) returns list rows with `value`; a keyed aggregate
lookup returns its scalar value. Query and mutation envelopes are distinct.

table notes:
  owner: principal
  title: text <=80
  public 'wire recipe'
  insert <- allow
  online only
view noteCounts: count notes by .owner
query list(c: cursor ?):
  require viewer = 'dev:alice' else READ_REFUSED

codex
I reproduced a second issue: witness mismatch logging awaits the proof-driver diagnostic binding before invoking the application mutation. Holding that binding unresolved prevented the write from running, contrary to the amendment’s guarantee that diagnostics never block application writes. I’m finishing the remaining source and qualification checks.

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#10 — packed starters must pass their real web toolchains
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { createServer } from "node:net";
import { Worker } from "node:worker_threads";
import { loadavg } from "node:os";
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { mkdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, existsSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { resolve, join } from "node:path";

// The live keeper alone signals its own group. Closing its private pipe also
// cleans up if this runner dies; no cached PID ever authorizes a signal.
export async function runToolchainCommand(command, args, { cwd, env, timeout }) {
  const owner = fileURLToPath(new URL("./test-command-owner.mjs", import.meta.url));
  const keeper = spawn(process.execPath, [owner, "100", command, ...args], { cwd, env, detached: true, stdio: ["pipe", "inherit", "inherit", "ipc"] });
  let released = false, completed = false, failure, status;
  const release = mode => { if (!released) { released = true; keeper.stdin.end(mode); } };
  const fail = error => { failure ??= error; release("TERM"); };
  const timer = setTimeout(() => fail(Error(`${command}: toolchain command timed out`)), timeout);
  keeper.stdin.on("error", error => { failure ??= error; });
  keeper.on("message", message => {
    if (message.error) fail(Error(message.error));
    if (message.exited) { completed = true; status = message.status; release("KILL"); }
  });
  await new Promise(done => {
    keeper.once("error", error => { failure ??= error; });
    keeper.once("close", done);
  });
  clearTimeout(timer);
  if (!released || !completed) failure ??= Error(`${command}: command owner exited without a result`);
  // This read-only group-existence probe needs no /proc mount in the clean
  // userspace. Refuse on lingering members or ambiguous/permission errors.
  // Two empty observations also protect against a transient inspection result.
  const deadline = performance.now() + 5_000;
  let empty = 0;
  if (keeper.pid) while (empty < 2) {
    try { process.kill(-keeper.pid, 0); empty = 0; }
    catch (error) { if (error.code === "ESRCH") empty++; else { failure ??= error; break; } }
    if (empty === 2) break;
    if (performance.now() >= deadline) { failure ??= Error(`${command}: toolchain process group did not drain`); break; }
    await sleep(50);
  }
  if (failure) throw failure;
  if (status !== 0) throw Error(`${command}: toolchain command exited ${status}`);
}
